  - *How to claim:* 1) Apply at education.github.com/pack (or github.com/education) with school email or student ID. 2) Approval unlocks the pack page at github.com/features/benefits/students. 3) Redeem each partner offer individually (Azure, M365, Heroku, domain, etc.).
  - *Expiry:* Benefits last while you remain a verified student (GitHub Pro, M365, JetBrains renew annually/on graduation). Heroku credit: fixed 24 months. Domain/SSL: 1 year. Once you graduate, the pack stops renewing.

  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack/
- **Microsoft 365 Education trial (30 days, schools) + free M365A license for institutions** (medium) — Microsoft offers schools a free 30-day Microsoft 365 Education trial (Word, Excel, PowerPoint, Teams, OneDrive, Class Notebook/whiteboard, Copilot in education apps, no credit card) claimable by an institution admin, and a free Microsoft 365 A1 (M365A) license for eligible K-12 schools and universities — through which most university students already get M365A at no personal cost via their school. - **Azure AI / Microsoft Foundry always-free tiers (available to students and startups alike)** (high) — From the Azure Free and Azure for Students pages read today: monthly always-free AI service amounts included with any free/12-month account: Azure Speech in Foundry Tools 0.5M neural characters/month; Document Intelligence 500 pages/month (S0); AI Search 50 MB storage (10k docs, 3 indexes); Vision in Foundry Tools 5,000 transactions/month per S1-S3 tier; Content Safety 5,000 text records or images/month; Azure DevOps free for 5 users; PostgreSQL/MySQL serverless free tiers. These stack on top of credit programs — useful for AI workloads while credits burn out.

  - *How to claim:* No separate claim — free amounts auto-attach to eligible Azure services; monitor usage in Cost Management to stay under caps.
  - *Expiry:* Always-free: perpetual. 12-month amounts: until 12 months from account creation, then standard pricing.
  - *Gotchas:* Usage above the free amounts bills at normal rates (with no card, it just throttles/stops). Large AI inference (e.g., LLM tokens) is NOT in the always-free list — paid per token even in Foundry; budget accordingly.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free
- **2025–26 change:** Material 2025-2026 changes vs 'known so far': (1) The Founders Hub is now a single-entry 'Microsoft for Startups' program (apply at startups.microsoft.com). The no-investor baseline is the self-serve Azure startup offer — $1,000 for 90 days, then up to $4,000 more (180 days) after business verification (= up to $5,000); the older '~$200 starter + $4,000' framing now appears only as the no-code 'Build your startup' entry in the application flow. Investor-referred startups now START at ~$100,000 and grow to up to $150,000 (previously 'up to $150k' was the headline). (2) 2025-2026 rebrand: 'Azure OpenAI' and 'Azure AI Services' are now 'Azure AI Foundry' / 'Microsoft Foundry' (sub-brands Foundry Tools / Foundry Control Plane / Foundry IQ); the Azure free-services list was refreshed to these names. (3) LLM frontier: there is NO free OpenAI/LLM tier — access is paid/application-based and is only covered 'free' via the $200/$100/$1,000-$5,000 credits. (4) Several Azure AI services' older 'Free (F0) resource tiers' were replaced by the current 'always-free' monthly allowances on the free-services page (e.g., Content Safety 5,000/mo, Speech 0.5M chars/mo); exact F0-retirement dates were NOT re-verified this session because the session's web-search budget was exhausted mid-task. Everything else above is confirmed from the official Microsoft pages read today (overview/application/FAQ dated 2026-05 to 2026-10; pricing + free-services + Azure for Students pages current as of 2026-10-22). No major offer has been fully sunset; the main shifts are the rebrand and the consolidation of the startup credit tiers into one program.
- **2025–26 change:** 2025-2026 program changes (verified via press + official MS pages): (1) July 1, 2025 — Microsoft for Startups restructure: the Founders Hub four-tier model (up to $150,000 in Azure credits, applied for with a validated business plan) was RETIRED, as were the 'Founders Hub' label, the Expert Network, and the Champion Network. It was replaced by a two-track system: a self-service track ($1,000 instantly for 90 days → up to $5,000 total for 180 days after business verification, for new Azure customers, credit card required for identity verification only) and an Investor Network track (VC/accelerator/university referral code; press-stated $100,000+ Azure credits, dedicated Microsoft POC, co-sell/Marketplace support). Pegasus (invitation-only dedicated Cloud Solutions Architect) was explicitly unchanged. Existing activated legacy credits stayed valid until their expiry, then auto-converted to pay-as-you-go; a Microsoft Q&A answer (July 2025) confirms non-investor applicants apply via the new 'Microsoft for Startups Credit Portal.' (2) June 2, 2026 — second update (official blog 'Microsoft for Startups: Apply and get credits to grow on Azure'): single entry point at startups.microsoft.com; startups 'start building immediately with Startup credits'; the up-to-$150,000 ceiling was RESTORED but now unlocked incrementally 'over time based on service adoption and sustained Azure usage and verified progress' rather than as a one-time grant; Investor Network partners unlock 'additional Startup credits, expanded support, and third-party benefits' earlier; current eligibility caps: not more than $350,000 lifetime free Azure credits, no Series C funding or later, private for-profit, HQ in an Azure region, no crypto mining; applications reviewed in ~3 business days (learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/microsoft-for-startups/overview, page updated 2026-08-05). (3) August 2025 — Azure Dev Tools for Teaching (successor to Microsoft Imagine) was DISCONTINUED: access disabled effective ~Aug 7-15, 2025; no new administrator signups after Aug 15, 2025; Microsoft provided NO direct replacement for educators — students' dev-tool benefits (incl. previously-bundled Visual Studio Pro) moved into the Azure for Students software-download list (now VS Community/Code-centric). Already-installed software keeps working. This is why the '12 months of Visual Studio Pro' line from the known-so-far notes no longer maps to any current Azure student offer (Pro/Enterprise IDE now comes via GitHub Student Developer Pack or Visual Studio Dev Essentials Premium). (4) The old 'Azure for College Students' offer ID (ms-azr-0170p) is still the legal offer terms page and matches today's $100/12-month/no-CC offer; no amount change in 2025-2026. (5) Not confirmed (could not re-verify today; pricing tables are JS-rendered and returned placeholders): exact Azure OpenAI free-trial token quotas and durations, and the Dev Essentials Premium $200/mo Azure-credit figure.
- **2025–26 change:** Verified structural changes as of 2026-10-22 (from pages fetched today): (1) The legacy 'Azure for Startups' program/pricing URLs are dead — azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/startup/ and /en-us/pricing/education now 404; the program is consolidated under 'Microsoft for Startups' at microsoft.com/en-us/startups with docs on learn.microsoft.com/startups (doc dates May–Oct 2026). (2) The milestone ladder from 'known so far' ($200 → $5K → $25K → $50K → $150K) is confirmed current, with details: $5K after business verification, $25K at 5+ workloads (~60 days) plus a one-time 2-year credit-use extension, $50K at 7+ workloads, $150K at 10+ workloads AND ~$3,000/month usage; tiers can be skipped (auto-evaluated, not sequential). (3) New/increased caps: standard member cap remains $150K, but Investor Network-backed startups can now unlock up to $200K in credits with expanded support (benefits page updated 2026-06-15); with an investor code the immediate starter credit is $1,000 (vs $200 without) per the application doc updated 2026-10-08. (4) Program is now explicitly limited to B2B software startups pre-seed→Series B (no Series C+), ≤$350K lifetime free Azure credits, HQ-country locked at sign-up, and personal-consumer Microsoft account (no work/school email). (5) GitHub Student Developer Pack (page © 2026): older standalone partner deals (e.g., multiple TLD domain offers) no longer listed; new AI-oriented partners (Camber, Boot.dev, ToDiagram) added; Copilot student tier now framed around 'GitHub AI Credits' with limited agent/chat. (6) Azure free account page shows no 2025/2026 policy changes — $200/30-day + 12-month free + always-free structure unchanged on the page fetched today. (7) Azure-for-faculty details and the M365 Education office-online page could not be re-fetched today (404s/timeouts), so no faculty-credit or free-Office change is asserted. Method note: this session's WebSearch budget was exhausted mid-task, so all verification is via direct WebFetch of official pages listed per finding.
- **2025–26 change:** TWO program sunsets in 2025-2026: (1) Effective ~June 27/July 1, 2025, the pre-2025 multi-tier Microsoft for Startups program — 'up to $150,000 in Azure credits' via L1 $1,000 / L2 $5,000 / L3 $25,000 / L4 $150,000 tiers, open to non-investor startups with a validated business plan — was retired and replaced by a two-track system: Investor Network track (~$100,000 start + additional awards, by investor referral) and a self-service track capped at $5,000 ($1,000 valid 90 days + $4,000 valid 180 days after business verification). Existing activated credits remained valid until expiry; no notice window, per The Register/TechRadar (July 1-2, 2025). The invitation-only Pegasus Program (dedicated Cloud Solutions Architect + Microsoft sales linkage) was reported unchanged in July 2025 but is not advertised anywhere in the 2026 MfS docs — likely quietly discontinued; confirm with MfS program support. (2) As of ~June 2, 2026, Microsoft replaced the two-track system with the current unified milestone model (see findings): anyone applies; $200 starter credits immediately (no investor code) or $1,000 (with investor code); up to $150,000 unlocked through business verification + sustained usage milestones (5/7/10 workloads, ~$3K/month). So the task's 'known so far' both figures are historical states: '$150,000' matches the pre-2025 program AND is the 2026 milestone ceiling; '~$100,000' matches the 2025 Investor-track start, not the current model. Marketing pages are lagging the docs: azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students still advertises the retired 2025 self-service track ('$1,000 instantly, up to $5,000, 90/180-day validity'), and the Founders Hub sign-up page still cites the legacy '$150,000 + $2,500 OpenAI credits' pitch; the official Learn docs (application page ms.date 2026-10-08) are the authoritative current state. Stale-URL note: https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/for-startups/ 404s today; the program now lives at microsoft.com/startups with docs under learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/. Note: the session's WebSearch budget was exhausted mid-task, so a few secondary items (MfS FAQ's exact URL, Azure for Students Starter per-service limits, Azure for Education page) were verified only indirectly and are marked medium/low confidence accordingly. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/microsoft-for-startups/application , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/microsoft-for-startups/getting-started-mfs , https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/blog/microsoft-for-startups-introduces-a-clearer-path-from-ai-development-to-enterprise-growth/ , https://www.theregister.com/off-prem/2025/07/01/microsoft-changes-conditions-for-azure-startup-credits/101398 , https://www.techradar.com/pro/microsoft-pulls-program-that-gave-generous-azure-boost-to-ai-startups , https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/ , https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students/ , https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/offers/ms-azr-0170p , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/education-hub/faq , https://education.github.com/pack , https://github.com/microsoft/AcademicContent/blob/main/offers-programs/README.md , https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/startups
- **2025–26 change:** No free offer for this front was sunset as of 2026-10-22, but several were materially changed in 2025-2026: (1) Microsoft for Startups was restructured into the tiered 'Founders Hub' model; the current headline is 'up to $150,000 in credits' (replacing the older 'up to $300,000 in total benefits' framing), with level-ups gated by an automated engagement score. (2) The live Cosmos DB 'free tier' is now the provisioned 1,000 RU/s + 25 GB allowance (confirmed on the current pricing page today); the previously documented separate 12-month serverless allowance (400,000 RU + 32 GB) does not appear on the current page, so plan against 1,000 RU/s + 25 GB. (3) The legacy Azure SQL Database 'free' provisioned F0 SKU has been retired/replaced by the perpetual serverless allowance (100,000 vCore-seconds/month + 32 GB storage) — if any docs still reference F0, they are stale. (4) Azure free account ($200 credit / 30 days + 12-month allowances) and Azure for Students ($100 credit / 12 months, no credit card) were unchanged in 2025-2026; note the student credit is $100, not $200 — $200 is the general new-customer trial. (5) Housekeeping: the dedicated SQL Database serverless pricing-details URL (azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/details/sql-database/serverless/) now returns 404 as of today; the free allowances are instead confirmed on the /free/ and /free/students pages, and Azure has rebranded much of its AI surface to 'Microsoft Foundry' (product-name changes only, no free-allowance changes observed).
- **2025–26 change:** 2025–2026 changes to be aware of: (1) The legacy '12-month free services + $100 credit' structure in the STANDARD free account no longer appears on the current official pages — the trial is now $200 for 30 days plus 12-month free amounts; the '$100 12-month credit' noted in 'known so far' appears outdated (medium confidence on the removal; not visible on /free or /pricing/free-services read today). (2) Microsoft for Startups was restructured on July 1, 2025 (memo June 27, 2025): the old tiered program letting UNFUNDED startups reach $150,000 (L1 $1,000 / L2 $5,000 / L3 $25,000 / L4 $150,000) was pulled; replaced by a two-track system — Investor Network track (referral from affiliated VC/accelerator/university, credits starting at $100,000) and a self-service track capped at $5,000 ($1,000/90 days + $4,000/180 days post-verification). Activated legacy credits remained valid until expiry. (3) The program changed again: for anyone who joined Microsoft for Startups after May 29, 2026, the Azure Portal becomes the primary experience and credits are milestone-based ($200 → $5k → $25k → $50k → $150k based on workload count and ~$3,000/month usage over 60 days; docs updated 2026-08-05), effectively re-opening the $150k ceiling via usage milestones rather than investor referral. (4) 'Azure for Teachers' ($1,000 credit for educators) has no current offer page — it was sunset years ago; faculty now use the standard $200/30-day free account (card required) or institution-level Microsoft Learn for Educators (MSLE, which channels the $100 student credit). (5) The 'Azure 6 months free for Visual Studio subscribers' developer offer page (azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/visual-studio) returns 404 — that offer appears discontinued as of today. (6) Azure Dev Tools for Teaching stopped accepting new administrator signups after August 15, 2025 (existing admins retain access). (7) Pegasus (invitation-only startup support) was explicitly unchanged by the 2025 restructure. (8) Many 'Foundry Tools' lines reflect the 2025–2026 Azure AI rebrand (Azure AI Services → Azure AI Foundry); amounts themselves are unchanged.
- **2025–26 change:** 1) Microsoft Education Portal (education.microsoft.com/portal — formerly the K-12 teacher/student portal that listed Microsoft 365 and Azure credit allowances) now 301-redirects (observed today) to the Microsoft Learn Educator Center, which lists no Azure/M365 credits; educator value has shifted to the Microsoft Elevate Educator/School programs and Microsoft Learn for Educators (year-round, tiered 'Explorer'/'Pathfinder' entry). The old portal's specific credit figures could not be verified today (page gone), so treat any pre-2025 "$25,000 M365 + $500 Azure credits" numbers as retired/low-confidence. 2) Startup program consolidation: the legacy Azure Startups Program (historically up to $300K credits with a 5-year company-age and $30M funding cap) and the 'Microsoft for Startups Founders' tier structure are replaced by the current single-entry Microsoft for Startups — cap expressed as 'up to $150K unlocked over time', eligibility now framed as 'no Series C or later' + '<$350K lifetime free Azure credits' rather than age/funding caps. 3) The 12-month free VM list now documents B1S + B2pts v2 (ARM) + B2ats v2 (AMD) burstable VMs at 750 hrs/month each; older doc generations listed B1s/B1ms/B2s — the ARM/AMD v2 SKUs are the current official set (confirmed on the Learn page dated 2026-03-03). 4) The student offer page no longer advertises a fixed '12 months of 25 services' list — it now says '$100 credit + select free services, subject to change' (Microsoft may end/alter it at discretion). 5) Method note: WebSearch budget was exhausted mid-task, so all verification used WebFetch of official pages; per-service always-free limit figures (Synapse 250h, Databricks 32 DBU, 100 GB egress) live in a JS-rendered table on the free-services page that could not be extracted and are marked medium confidence.
- **2025–26 change:** 1) July 1, 2025 (announced June 27, 2025): the classic Founders Hub multi-tier program (apply-then-upgrade to $100 → $5,000 → $25,000 → $150,000 with business-plan validation) was RETIRED and replaced by a two-track system: Investor Network track ($100,000+ starting credits, referral from affiliated investors/accelerators) and a self-serve track ($1,000 for 90 days + $4,000 for 180 days after business verification, cap $5,000 without investor affiliation). Existing activated credits of old participants stayed valid until expiry; unfunded participants' accounts converted to pay-as-you-go after expiry. Pegasus (invitation-only, dedicated Cloud Solutions Architect) continued but out of the new funnel. 2) 2026 restructure (MFS 2.0, docs dated May 2026): the program was rebranded 'Microsoft for Startups' with a single entry and automatic milestone upgrades: $200 immediate → $5,000 → $25,000 → $50,000 → $150,000, with the $150K tier gated on 10+ concurrent workloads and ~$3,000/month sustained Azure spend. Eligibility also tightened: funding cap moved from 'not Series D or later' to 'not Series C or later', and a $350,000 lifetime-free-Azure-credits cap was added. So the oft-quoted 'no funding required, up to $150K' is now true only in the narrow sense that early stages need no funding, but Series C+ companies are excluded and $150K requires heavy sustained Azure spend. 3) Legacy partner credits from the old Founders Hub ($2,500 OpenAI, Databricks, Twilio $10,000, Atlassian, etc.) are no longer listed in the current FAQ (read today); the signup-page snapshot still showed 'up to $150,000 in Azure credits, plus $2,500 of OpenAI credits' — treat OpenAI/Databricks/Twilio partner credits as unconfirmed/sunset unless shown in-portal; current investor-track benefits are GitHub Enterprise, LinkedIn Premium Business, M365 Business Premium, Visual Studio Enterprise, Dynamics 365, Power Platform. 4) GitHub Student Developer Pack: DigitalOcean's $200 credit offer left the pack (credits expire 2026-08-01); Bump.sh offer ended 2025-12-09; Appwrite restructured to 6 months (2026-10-02); GitHub Foundations certification voucher was paused 2025-10-25 then resumed 2025-09-30. 5) The Azure Free page no longer advertises the old '6 months free AI services' window — only 12-month free amounts and always-free tiers.
- **Critic correction** ($100 in Azure credits, 12 months of Visual Studio Pro): The $100 credit is valid for 12 months. Visual Studio Professional is not in the included product list, which names Visual Studio Code, Visual Studio Community, Visual Studio for Mac, Agents for Visual Studio, Remote Tools for Visual Studio and Visio Professional. The 12-month Cloud Academy benefit comes with paid Visual Studio Professional subscriptions, not Azure for Students.

### Oracle Cloud Always Free

- **OCI Always Free tier (VPS: 2 AMD micro VMs + 2 OCPU/12 GB ARM Ampere A1 pool + storage/DB/networking)** (high) — Perpetual ('life of the account') Always Free resources, home region only: (1) up to 2 VM.Standard.E2.1.Micro AMD instances, each 1/8 OCPU (~200 MHz, burstable) + 1 GB RAM, 1 VNIC, 1 public IPv4, up to 50 Mbps internet / 480 Mbps intra-region; (2) Ampere A1 ARM (VM.Standard.A1.Flex): a monthly pool of 1,500 OCPU-hours + 9,000 GB-hours = equivalent to 2 OCPUs and 12 GB total, usable as one instance or up to two 1-OCPU instances (min boot volume 47 GB/instance); (3) 200 GB block volume total (boot + data, home region) with 5 volume backups; (4) object storage: 20 GB combined Standard+Infrequent Access+Archive for free-only tenancies (10 GB per tier while trial/PAYG is active); (5) 10 TB/month outbound egress; (6) 2x Autonomous AI Database (20 GB storage each, 1 Oracle CPU, serverless Exadata, 20 max concurrent sessions); (7) MySQL HeatWave standalone single-node cluster, 50 GB data + 50 GB backup storage; (8) NoSQL: 133M reads + 133M writes/mo, 3 tables x 25 GB (Phoenix region only); (9) 2 VCNs, 1 Flexible Load Balancer + 1 Network Load Balancer, 50 IPsec site-to-site VPN connections, 5 CAs + 150 certificates, 3,000 emails/mo Email Delivery, plus free APEX, Resource Manager/Terraform, Monitoring/Logging, Bastion, Vault (software keys unlimited; 20 HSM key versions), Security Advisor, Content Management Starter. NO free bare-metal or dedicated-server shapes exist — OCI bare metal (BM.Dense/BM.Standard, incl. 80-core Ampere bare metal) is paid-only.
  - *Eligibility:* Open to anyone who signs up (one free trial/Always Free account per person; one cloud account per email). Credit or debit card REQUIRED at signup: major-network cards (Visa/Mastercard/Discover/Amex) that do not require a PIN; virtual, prepaid, single-use and PIN-debit cards are rejected. A mobile phone number is needed for most users. Available in all regions where commercial OCI is offered (Free/Always Free NOT available in US Government Cloud regions); '$300 credit' part is 'available in select countries'. .edu email NOT required for the free tier. Startup age/funding caps: none for the free tier.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at https://signup.oraclecloud.com (linked from https://www.oracle.com/cloud/free/): select country, enter name + valid email, verify email (link valid 30 min), set password (8-40 chars, complexity rules), optionally enter company name, create a cloud account name, pick Home Region (irrevocable; Always Free compute + ADB can only be created in the home region), enter address + phone, add credit/debit card as payment-verification method (you may see a small temporary ~$1 verification hold that auto-reverses), accept ToU, 'Start my free trial'. Always Free resources are usable immediately with no upgrade; $0 charged unless you upgrade to PAYG. No separate claim form for the Always Free tier itself.
  - *Expiry:* Perpetual — 'available for an unlimited period of time, for the life of the account.' Trial portion: $300 credits expire 30 days after signup. No renewal needed.
  - *Gotchas:* HOME-REGION LOCK: Always Free compute and ADB provision only in home region; use in other regions bills at PAYG rates. IDLE RECLAMATION: Always Free VMs (and bare metal) can be reclaimed when over any 7-day window CPU p95 < 20% AND network < 20% (AND memory < 20% for A1 shapes). Accounts idle 30+ days may be deemed abandoned and suspended/terminated. 2026 ARM CUT-ENFORCEMENT: since 2026-08-18, A1 instances exceeding 2 OCPU/12 GB are auto-terminated; once an existing oversized instance is terminated it 'may not be possible' to recreate it above the new limit. Capacity: free-tier accounts frequently hit 'out of host capacity' on A1/micro shapes in US regions; PAYG upgrade generally provisions more reliably. Eligible OS images limited (Oracle Linux/Ubuntu/CentOS for micros; Oracle Linux/Ubuntu + Oracle Linux Cloud Developer for A1, latter needs >=8 GB). No SLA and no Oracle Support unless a paid account. If trial ends with more A1 OCPUs/memory than 2/12, excess instances are disabled then deleted after 30 days unless you upgrade. Paid resources made with trial credits are deleted (unrecoverable) if you don't upgrade.
  - *Source:* https://docs.oracle.com/en-us/iaas/Content/FreeTier/freetier_topic-Always_Free_Resources.htm(alsohttps://www.oracle.com/cloud/free/)—bothread2026-10-22
- **OCI Free Trial: US$300 cloud credit for 30 days (all eligible OCI services, on top of Always Free)** (high) — One-time US$300 in cloud credits valid up to 30 days from account creation, spendable on all eligible OCI services (compute beyond free limits, GPU, K8s, analytics, up to ~8 instances / 5 TB storage while credits last, per the trial marketing). Credits are consumed at discounted trial rates. Unused credits expire at 30 days. After trial: 30-day grace to upgrade; paid resources are otherwise reclaimed/deleted; Always Free resources persist. Upgrading carries a ~$100 card authorization. Extensions/additional credits possible only via an Oracle sales representative (console Upgrade page).
  - *Eligibility:* Same as free tier signup: one per person; credit/debit card (major network, no PIN required) + phone number for identity verification; US$300 credit 'available in select countries' per the FAQ asterisk. No .edu, no startup-age requirement — this is a general trial, not a special program.
  - *How to claim:* Automatically granted at https://signup.oraclecloud.com — no separate claim. Steps: country -> name -> email (verify within 30 min) -> password -> optional company -> cloud account name -> Home Region -> address/phone -> add card verification (small temporary ~$1 hold) -> ToU -> 'Start my free trial'. Upgrade to PAYG at any time via Console > Billing & Cost Management > Upgrade and Manage Payment.
  - *Expiry:* Credits: 30 days from signup or $300 exhaustion, whichever first. Trial resources: 30-day grace after expiry to upgrade before deletion. Always Free portion: no expiry.
  - *Gotchas:* Card IS required (contrary to 'no credit card' marketing assumptions): FAQ says credit cards and PIN-less debit cards only; virtual/prepaid/single-use cards rejected; periodic re-validation holds possible. 'Some special Free Trial offers' (e.g., email-invite promotions): after expiry the account is suspended for 30 days then permanently deleted — they do NOT convert to an Always Free account. No SLA/support once credits gone. Billing begins after trial if you upgraded (credit balance preserved until trial period ends). No downgrade from paid back to free. Data in trial-created resources is unrecoverable after reclamation.
  - *Source:* https://www.oracle.com/cloud/free/faq/andhttps://docs.oracle.com/en-us/iaas/Content/FreeTier/freetier.htm(bothread2026-10-22);signupsteps:https://docs.oracle.com/en-us/iaas/Content/GSG/Tasks/signingup_topic-Sign_Up_for_Free_Oracle_Cloud_Promotion.htm
- **Oracle Startup Program (startup cloud credits)** (low) ⚠ low confidence: verify before claiming — UNVERIFIABLE TODAY. The global Oracle startups section at https://www.oracle.com/startups/ (and /accelerate/) returned HTTP 403 (Oracle edge firewall 'fw_error_www') to all non-browser clients on 2026-10-22, so exact credit amounts, funding caps, and application terms could not be read. Historical/known program: 'Oracle Startup Program' / Oracle Accelerate, where eligible early-stage startups apply for additional OCI cloud credits beyond the $300 trial; regional Oracle sites (uk/europe/latam/middleeast/japan) have NO startups section — /startups/ URLs there 301-redirect to /cloud/ or 404, indicating the program is global-only and likely gated behind the bot-protected global page. Exact amounts/duration are not confirmed in this pass; treat any number as unverified.
  - *Eligibility:* Unknown today (page inaccessible). Historically: early-stage startups (founded within recent years, limited funding raised); application through the Oracle startups portal; personal accounts are not eligible. $300 trial and Always Free tier are available to startups in their personal capacity regardless.
  - *How to claim:* If accessible: application form at https://www.oracle.com/startups/ (company details; program-specific credit grant); fallback: standard $300/30-day trial at signup.oraclecloud.com. Because the page blocked automated access, verify manually in a browser.
  - *Expiry:* Unknown (unverified).
  - *Gotchas:* Page blocked by Oracle's WAF for automated fetchers — confirm in a real browser before relying on any numbers; do not cite the 'known so far' 2025-era figures without re-verification; no startup program details appear in docs.oracle.com Free Tier docs, so nothing in official docs corroborates it.
  - *Source:* https://www.oracle.com/startups/(inaccessibleviaautomation2026-10-22,HTTP403)
- **Education offer: no dedicated .edu cloud-credit program; free tier open to students/academics + free Oracle University training/certifications (Oracle Learning Explorer)** (medium) — No .edu-gated OCI VPS license found on official pages as of 2026-10-22. The free tier FAQ explicitly targets 'a student wanting to learn, or an academic developing curriculum in the cloud' — students simply use the standard Always Free + $300/30-day trial (no .edu email required; the signup email is only used to check 'if you are eligible for any special offers', so education special offers can appear at signup if targeted). Free education/training: (a) Oracle Learning Explorer — free entry-level training courses and accreditation across Oracle's product portfolio; (b) Oracle University 'Free Foundations training' — free training and certifications for OCI, Oracle Fusion Cloud Applications and AI (oracle.com/education/training, 'New free Foundations training... free training and certifications'); (c) a promo on that page: 'Free certification on OCI, AI, multicloud, and data services — Now through October 31, join the Race to Certification 2025' (note: page footer is © 2026, the 'Race to Certification 2025' banner looks like stale promotional copy — the offer may have already expired on 2025-10-31; do not count on it without re-checking).
  - *Eligibility:* Training: open to anyone, no .edu email, no card. Cloud: standard free-tier eligibility (one account/person, card + phone). .edu email NOT required anywhere.
  - *How to claim:* Cloud: standard signup at signup.oraclecloud.com (any special education offer is surfaced there via the email eligibility check). Training: enroll at mylearn.oracle.com / oracle.com/education/training ('Explore free training and certifications'); Learning Explorer via oracle.com/education ('Start learning for free').
  - *Expiry:* Free Foundations training: ongoing/promotional ('New free Foundations training' as of the page's © 2026). Race to Certification: 'through October 31' (year unclear — likely already ended 2025-10-31; stale banner).
  - *Gotchas:* Oracle University free tier is training-only, no infrastructure credits; the 'free' training does not include the full exam voucher unless the specific Foundations course lists it; verify 'Race to Certification' status in the console/marketing site before assuming it's live; no .edu-only benefits exist in OCI as of this check.
  - *Source:* https://www.oracle.com/education/training/andhttps://www.oracle.com/cloud/free/faq/(bothread2026-10-22)
- **2025–26 change:** Material 2025-2026 changes: (1) ALWAYS FREE AMPERE A1 HALVED, SILENTLY — Oracle's docs changed without a public announcement so that the A1 pool went from 3,000 OCPU-hours + 18,000 GB-hours/month (equivalent to 4 OCPU / 24 GB, i.e., 'up to four Ampere instances' as the older MySQL-free page still claims) to 1,500 OCPU-hours + 9,000 GB-hours/month (= 2 OCPU / 12 GB total, 'one or two instances, 2 OCPUs total'). Per InfoQ (2026-07-03) the docs change took effect 2026-06-15 with no blog post or customer notice; hard enforcement (auto-termination of over-limit instances) began 2026-08-18 per a customer email quoted by CNELECAR. Ambiguity remains: Oracle support reportedly told PAYG tenancies the old 4/24 limits still apply free, while the docs say 'All tenancies get the first 1,500 OCPU hours...' — unconfirmed either way. Once an oversized A1 instance is terminated it likely cannot be recreated above the new limit. AMD micros, 200 GB block, object storage, 10 TB egress, and 2x20 GB ADB limits are unchanged. (2) $300/30-DAY TRIAL — unchanged through 2025-2026; card still required at signup. (3) STARTUP PROGRAM — not verifiable this pass (bot-blocked global page); no evidence it was sunset, but no official docs corroborate it either. (4) BARE-METAL / DEDICATED-SERVER FRONT — Oracle has no free bare-metal or dedicated-server offer at any tier; bare-metal/dedicated is paid-only (PAYG or Always-Free-exempt). (5) EDUCATION — no .edu-gated program currently advertised; free Foundations training/certifications remain available per the ©2026 Oracle University pages.

### OVHcloud Free Trial

- **Public Cloud Free Trial (global, www.ovhcloud.com) — US$200 / 1 month** (high) — US$200 free trial credit (displayed in local currency of the account's market/country, ex-VAT) valid for one month, activated when the first Public Cloud project is created. Applies to any Public Cloud service in the catalogue across all regions where Public Cloud is offered, incl. GPU/AI: the page's worked example covers 35 hours of AI1-1-GPU AI Notebook and 5 hours of AI Training on 4 AI1-1-GPU nodes. One voucher per natural or legal person (even across multiple NIC handles). Non-refundable, non-transferable, no combining with other offers; if the credit outlives on another NIC handle OVHcloud may terminate services; amounts covered by the voucher are excluded from SLA calculation. Offer live since 30 Jun 2022.
  - *Eligibility:* Any OVHcloud customer (new or existing) who has NEVER created a Public Cloud project (active or past). A valid payment method (credit card/bank) must already be saved in the account before starting — a card IS required, contrary to some listings. No .edu/education email requirement. Available in all regions where Public Cloud is offered. One per person/entity.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create/log in to an OVHcloud account (ovhcloud.com). 2) Ensure a payment method is saved. 3) In the Control Panel open Public Cloud and create your first project — the FREETRIAL voucher activates on project creation. 4) Deploy resources; the US$200 is auto-deducted from that project's bills for one month. 5) After the month or when the credit runs out, the saved payment method is billed for further usage.
  - *Expiry:* Voucher valid 1 month from project activation; non-renewable, non-exchangeable. After expiry the account reverts to standard pay-as-you-go billing.
  - *Gotchas:* It's a trial credit, not a free tier — payment method required and unused credit vanishes after 30 days. VPS and bare-metal/dedicated servers are NOT covered (Public Cloud only); the trial page's own examples show GPU AI notebooks/AI training. Third-party trackers (agentdeals.dev) recorded a terms tightening on 2026-04-01 and a 2026-10-01 price change (Gen3 B3/C3/R3 instances now bill local storage and a public IPv4 as separate lines, raising total bills 1.4–21.9% for small instances), which makes the US$200 burn faster on Gen3 instances.
  - *Source:* https://www.ovhcloud.com/en/public-cloud/free-trial/
- **Public Cloud Free Trial (OVHcloud US, us.ovhcloud.com) — up to $300 / 90 days** (high) — US$200 credit when creating the first US Public Cloud project PLUS an additional $100 credit for creating the first Managed Database service — up to $300 total. Credits valid 90 days from the date they are applied (applied automatically; operational target 2 business days, contractual outer bound 30 days after OVHcloud receives the required $0.99 purchase). Cover Public Cloud only: compute (VMs, Cloud GPU, Local Zones), Block/Object/File storage, network, Managed Kubernetes, managed databases (MySQL, PostgreSQL, MongoDB, Valkey, Kafka, OpenSearch, Grafana), AI & ML (AI Notebooks, AI Training, AI Deploy) and security/ops services. Do NOT cover bare metal, VPS or Hosted Private Cloud.
  - *Eligibility:* US datacenters only; customers who have not previously purchased Public Cloud services from OVHcloud. Must purchase at least $0.99 of Public Cloud services to trigger; a payment method with a $0.99 fee must be added when creating the first project — i.e. a card is required (though effectively cost-free via the $0.99 charge). No promo code, no .edu requirement.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign up for an OVHcloud US account (us.ovhcloud.com). 2) Create your first Public Cloud project in the Control Panel — add a payment method ($0.99 fee applies). 3) Launch your first resource ($0.99 purchase triggers eligibility). 4) The $200 credit auto-applies within 2 business days; buy your first Managed Database service to unlock the extra $100. 5) Check balance in the account's Payment Methods > vouchers tab.
  - *Expiry:* Credits expire 90 days after being applied to the account. No renewal/extension; unusable balance is void. After expiry the account bills at standard rates via the default payment method.
  - *Gotchas:* The page's header says 'one-month free trial' but the terms state 90-day validity — treat 90 days as contractual. Charges continue automatically once credits are exhausted/expired via the card on file. US-only infrastructure: US regions with Public Cloud availability qualify. The $100 Managed-DB bonus is the part most easily missed.
  - *Source:* https://us.ovhcloud.com/public-cloud/free-trial/
- **OVHcloud Startup Program — Start level: €10,000 credits / 12 months (EU; $12,000 US)** (high) — 12-month membership: €10,000 in free cloud credits (US program: up to $12,000; Canada site shows $14,000) usable on Public Cloud and Hosted Private Cloud, plus 6 hours of free 1-on-1 engineering consultation, regional manager access, free social visibility ('Shine On'), Showcase events with VCs, mentoring. Credits are vouchers valid 12 months; unused vouchers are lost at the end. Voucher-eligible products: Public Cloud compute (instances incl. GPUs — only V100, V100s and RTX5000 GPU instances, all other GPUs excluded), Block/Object/Cold Archive storage, Managed K8s, managed databases, AI & ML (AI Notebooks, AI Training, AI Deploy, AI Endpoints), Quantum Notebooks; Hosted Private Cloud VMware/Nutanix. Not eligible: VPS, dedicated/bare-metal servers, Web Cloud, telecom. Also: quantum emulators get 'free credits via the Startup Program or free trial offer' per the QPU product page. No equity taken, no exclusivity (competing programs allowed).
  - *Eligibility:* Selected pre-seed and seed startups with at least a Proof of Concept or MVP deployable on OVHcloud, and a professional startup/business activity. Excluded: NGOs, associations, resellers/integrators, crypto mining, black-hat SEO, gaming bots, companies that already joined the program (incl. former Digital Launch Pad). No published company-age cap and no published funding cap for Start (OVHcloud decides the tier; any prior funding round 'improves chances' but isn't required; pre-revenue and revenue-generating both apply). Geographic: apply on the country site matching your business address; US infrastructure companies use the separate US program with a US account; generally one program membership (country of incorporation/operation). No .edu email; individual students aren't the target — it's company-level.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Pick the right country site at startup.ovhcloud.com (or us.ovhcloud.com/startup-program for US). 2) Click Apply and link an existing OVHcloud customer account or create a new one (use a business account, not personal). 3) Answer company questions; you may get an email requesting more info. 4) Decision within ~7 days. 5) On acceptance sign the program contract in the Control Panel; vouchers arrive within ~48 hours; a regional program manager onboards you. Spend vouchers on eligible services during the 12 months.
  - *Expiry:* Vouchers must be used within 12 months of the contract; remaining balance is automatically invalidated. No renewal; one membership per company, ever (re-applying after exit not allowed).
  - *Gotchas:* Credits can't pay for VPS or dedicated servers — the 'fronts: VPS' angle only works through Public Cloud instances/Bare Metal Cloud (Bare Metal Cloud IS listed eligible in OVH docs' eligible-products table, while classic 'Dedicated Servers' and 'VPS' are not). GPU access under the program is limited to V100/V100s/RTX5000 instances. Trial-vs-free: this is a selective, application-based credit grant, not self-serve. Application must be honest — false/vague info gets it rejected. If you order non-eligible products by mistake they bill your regular payment method.
  - *Source:* https://startup.ovhcloud.com/en/startups/
- **OVHcloud Startup Program — Scale level: up to €100,000 credits / 12 months (EU; up to $120,000 US)** (medium) — 12 months for Series A (and beyond) funded startups/scaleups: vouchers sized by OVHcloud's assessment of your expected 12-month infrastructure spend, capped at €100,000 (US: up to $120,000), plus up to 20 hours of 1-on-1 engineer time, Business-tier support, personalized quotes via Sales, personal visibility and market/funding event access. Same voucher eligibility rules as Start level (Public Cloud + Hosted Private Cloud; GPU only V100/V100s/RTX5000; no VPS/dedicated servers; AI Notebooks/Training/Deploy and Quantum Notebooks eligible). 'Enabler' route: accelerators/incubators/VCs that are OVHcloud partners can bring members to the same ceilings (up to €100k/20h); an event code is only mandatory when the support structure is a Premium partner.
  - *Eligibility:* Startups/scaleups that have raised Series A or later (funding stage is the main discriminator vs Start; OVHcloud makes the final tier decision). Same exclusions as Start level (NGOs, non-business projects, resellers, contentious activities, prior program members). Company site matching business address; US program requires a US OVHcloud account.
  - *How to claim:* Same as Start level: apply online at startup.ovhcloud.com (country site per your address) or us.ovhcloud.com/startup-program, link/create a business OVHcloud account, respond to follow-up, ~7-day decision, sign contract in Control Panel, vouchers within ~48h, manager onboarding.
  - *Expiry:* Vouchers valid 12 months from contract; unused balance lost. Participation capped at 12 months per level.
  - *Gotchas:* 'Up to' — actual amount is set by OVHcloud from your projected spend, not guaranteed at the cap. Series A+ requirement means most seed-stage teams get the €10k Start level instead. No exclusivity with other cloud providers' programs. GPU instances other than V100/V100s/RTX5000 aren't voucher-eligible even at Scale level.
  - *Source:* https://startup.ovhcloud.com/en/scaleups/
- **Public VCF-as-a-Service first month free (US promo, code VCFTRIAL)** (medium) — Limited-time promotion on OVHcloud US homepage: first month of Public VCF-as-a-Service (VMware Cloud Foundation workloads on shared infrastructure) free for new OVHcloud customers, using code VCFTRIAL at checkout. Standard Starter Pack only. This is a VMware-hosted private-cloud offering, not a GPU/VPS/dedicated-server product.
  - *Eligibility:* New OVHcloud US customers only; first 50 customers or until October 31, 2026, whichever comes first (i.e. it ends ~9 days after the 2026-10-22 reference date). No .edu requirement; payment at checkout after the free month.
  - *How to claim:* 1) New OVHcloud US account. 2) Order Public VCF-as-a-Service Standard Starter Pack. 3) Enter code VCFTRIAL at checkout to waive the first month.
  - *Expiry:* Offer limited to first 50 customers or 31 Oct 2026. After the free month, standard pricing auto-renews unless cancelled before the promotional period ends.
  - *Gotchas:* Auto-renews to paid — cancel if you don't want it. Strict customer cap (50). The promo is US-only and VMware-specific; unrelated to the $200/$300 Public Cloud trial or the Startup Program (don't assume it can be stacked — page says 'additional terms apply').
  - *Source:* https://us.ovhcloud.com/
- **Student/education offer: free access to OVHcloud quantum notebooks (Qualifying Students program)** (low) ⚠ low confidence: verify before claiming — Announced with the Quandela MosaiQ QPU inauguration (2024; rooted in the 2023 C12 notebook release): selected students from higher-education/research institutions (incl. QuanTEdu-France consortium members such as Telecom Paris) receive free access to OVHcloud's full quantum notebook range (Alice & Bob, C12, Eviden, Pasqal, Quandela, later Qiskit) and to the Quandela QPU via the cloud — original press announced 80 hours of notebook access per selected student. OVHcloud's current QPU product page says free emulation credits are 'available... particularly for startups, researchers or innovative companies — via the OVHcloud Startup Program or the free trial offer', i.e. students effectively ride the Startup Program/trial credits rather than a dedicated self-serve .edu offer.
  - *Eligibility:* Students at qualifying higher-education/research institutions; selection by application review (2024 program cited QuanTEdu-France/Telecom Paris). No .edu self-serve tier is published on ovhcloud.com today. Adjacent non-free education channel: OCRE 2024 framework (awarded to OVHcloud by GÉANT, 5-year agreement running since Feb 2025) gives research/education institutions in 9 European countries (BE, FR, DE, IT, NL, PL, PT, ES, UK) simplified procurement access to OVHcloud Public Cloud — negotiated framework terms, not free credits.
  - *How to claim:* No self-serve claim path published. Route: get your student/employer enrolled as an OVHcloud customer (e.g. via a startup/researcher account or OCRE-eligible institution) and use the US$200 Public Cloud free trial or apply to the Startup Program; request access to quantum notebooks/emulators in that account.
  - *Expiry:* No published expiry; the 2024 '80 hours' figure was per selected student. OCRE 2024 framework runs ~5 years from Feb 2025.
  - *Gotchas:* Low confidence this is still actively recruiting individual students — it was an announced ecosystem initiative, not advertised on current ovhcloud.com marketing pages as of 2026-10-22. Quantum notebook access is metered per-minute like other AI notebooks unless covered by trial/program credits. Don't count on a free .edu tier for VPS/dedicated servers — none exists.
  - *Source:* https://corporate.ovhcloud.com/en/newsroom/news/inauguration-quantum-computer/
- **2025–26 change:** As of 2026-10-22: (1) The legacy offer in the task's 'known so far' — 'US$200 free trial on new dedicated servers & VPS, 2-month, no commitment (refundable)' — is GONE: https://www.ovhcloud.com/en/free-trial/ returns 404; the only live trial is the Public Cloud US$200/1-month trial (and up to $300/90 days in the US). No dedicated-server or VPS trial credit is advertised anywhere on ovhcloud.com or us.ovhcloud.com. (2) 2026-04-01: third-party changelog (agentdeals.dev) records OVHcloud narrowing the free-trial terms; and on 2026-10-01 OVHcloud restructured Gen3 Public Cloud instance pricing — local storage and public IPv4 on B3/C3/R3 are now billed as separate lines (~€109/TB/mo storage, ~$1.97/mo IPv4), raising bills 1.4–21.9% (largest for small instances), which consumes trial credits faster. (3) VPS pricing: VPS 2026 list prices were raised 36–49% effective 2026-04-01 (e.g. VPS-1 €4.49→€6.49/mo); the new 'VPS 2027' range launched (VPS-1 2027 from ~$4.54/mo per www.ovhcloud.com/en/vps/). No VPS free tier/trial exists. (4) Bare metal: Kimsufi budget range was discontinued and replaced by the 'Eco' range (~$30/mo, us.ovhcloud.com/deals); bare-metal prices rose 9–11% for new deployments post-April 2026. (5) The VCFTRIAL first-month-free VMware promo is a sunset-in-9-days offer (ends 31 Oct 2026 or after first 50 customers). (6) No dedicated .edu/students free tier and no 'education license' for VPS/dedicated servers was found; the only education-adjacent channels are the qualifying-student quantum notebook program (2024, low visibility now) and the OCRE 2024 GÉANT procurement framework (institutional, negotiated, not free). 7) Startup Program structure is unchanged in substance (€10k Start / up to €100k Scale, 12 months) but currency values differ by country site (US $12k/$120k, CA $14k).

### Scaleway Startup

- **New-account free trial: €100 credit (Business/Professional account)** (high) — Create a Business account (older pages say Professional) and get €100 free credit across cloud services. The trial does not auto-apply: if eligible, a voucher code is emailed within 24 hours and the trial starts when you redeem it. Credit expiry is not officially stated. Payment method is required: ordering resources needs a valid credit card (€1 authorization is placed and auto-reimbursed within 2 days); virtual prepaid cards are not accepted; KYC identity verification is part of the account process.
  - *Eligibility:* New Business/Professional customers with a valid payment method who have NEVER had an invoice issued by Scaleway (a €0 invoice counts as an invoice and disqualifies you). Not advertised for Personal accounts. No .edu or startup-status requirement; effectively open to any new account.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create an account at console.scaleway.com, choosing a Business (formerly 'Professional') organization type. 2) Add a valid credit card (Visa/MasterCard/CB/UnionPay/Amex; SEPA with a physical card on file). 3) Wait for the voucher-code email (within 24h of qualifying) and redeem it in the console to activate the €100 trial.
  - *Expiry:* Official expiry not stated; voucher must be redeemed (emailed within 24h). Credit not cash-outable.
  - *Gotchas:* Any prior Scaleway invoice (even €0) disqualifies — a forgotten test account silently blocks the offer. Virtual/prepaid cards rejected. GPU is not excluded from the credit, but new-account quotas may limit larger GPU types. Dedicated-server and some products bill separately; egress/data transfer rules vary per product.
  - *Source:* https://www.scaleway.com/en/scaleway-vs-aws/(trialFAQ+'€100freecredit'banner;mirroredonhttps://www.scaleway.com/en/generative-apis-signup/andhttps://www.scaleway.com/en/scaleway-vs-digitalocean/)
- **Startup Program — Founders tier: €1,000 credit, 12 months** (high) — Entry tier of Scaleway's Startup Program for startups migrating to Scaleway or doing an initial build. €1,000 in cloud credits valid 12 months, not renewable; support is self-serve (no dedicated CSM); benefits include collective onboarding, private Slack with peers/alumni, product betas, masterclasses, co-marketing, and VC-network access. Application review takes under 21 business days; admission is rolling.
  - *Eligibility:* Entity under 5 years old; fewer than 50 employees; must NOT already be a Scaleway customer. No funding-stage or location cap stated. Open regardless of vertical.
  - *How to claim:* Apply via the Startup Program application (scaleway.com/en/startup-program/ or email startup-program@scaleway.com). Committee reviews applications; program dates are set per cohort (6 or 12 months after credits activation).
  - *Expiry:* Credits valid 12 months; non-renewable. Overage beyond the voucher is billed.
  - *Gotchas:* Being an existing customer (even a trial account) disqualifies you — coordinate with the €100 trial so an invoice isn't issued first. No funding cap published, but committee discretion applies; 'up to €1,000' vs '€1,000' phrasing differs between the nav and the benefits block.
  - *Source:* https://www.scaleway.com/en/startup-program/founders-program/
- **Startup Program — Early Stage tier: €1,500/month x 6 = up to €9,000** (high) — For startups going to market at pre-seed to seed stage. €1,500 per month of cloud credits over 6 months (total €9,000), with community support, co-marketing, and technical guidance from a CSM on request. Implied consumption floor of €500/month (the FAQ states this tier starts at '500€/month consumption').
  - *Eligibility:* Less than 5 years old, fewer than 50 employees, not yet a Scaleway client; pre-seed to seed stage. Cannot move from Early Stage directly into Growth Stage.
  - *How to claim:* Same rolling application as the program (scaleway.com/en/startup-program/, or startup-program@scaleway.com); selected startups join a cohort running 6 months from credit activation.
  - *Expiry:* 6-month credit window; overage beyond the voucher billed.
  - *Gotchas:* Consumption floor (~€500/month) means this is a ramp-up grant, not a small grant; usage below the floor is not expected. Page says 'up to €9,000' and '1 500€ per month for 6 month'.
  - *Source:* https://www.scaleway.com/en/startup-program/
- **Startup Program — Growth Stage tier: €3,000/month x 12 = up to €36,000** (high) — For seed-to-Series-A startups scaling up. €3,000 per month of cloud credits over 12 months (total €36,000), with dedicated CSM, 24/7 technical support (page typo '27/7'), co-marketing, events, and VC-network access. Implied consumption floor of €1,500/month.
  - *Eligibility:* Less than 5 years old, fewer than 50 employees, not yet a Scaleway client; seed to Series A stage. An Nvidia-dedicated variant has conditions similar to Growth.
  - *How to claim:* Apply via scaleway.com/en/startup-program/ (rolling admission; a page FAQ says the committee reviews weekly/monthly — see gotchas). 12-month cohort from credit activation.
  - *Expiry:* 12-month credit window; overage billed.
  - *Gotchas:* Highest exposure — floor of ~€1,500/month; the €36,000 only materializes if consumption matches the monthly tranche. Program FAQ wording conflicts internally on review cadence (weekly on the main page, monthly on the Founders page FAQ, 'under 21 business days' on Founders).
  - *Source:* https://www.scaleway.com/en/startup-program/
- **Object Storage trial: 750 GB free for 90 days (new users)** (high) — New users get 750 GB of free storage total, splittable between Standard Multi-AZ and Standard One Zone classes, across all regions, for 90 days. If you exceed 750 GB during the trial, only the excess is billed. Trial is activated from the Object Storage section of the console and cannot be paused/restarted once started; after 90 days regular pricing applies.
  - *Eligibility:* New users who had never used the Standard Multi-AZ class before 30 Nov 2023 23:59:59 (stated in the official FAQ). No card needed specifically for the trial itself, though ordering resources generally requires a valid payment method.
  - *How to claim:* Activate in the Scaleway console under Object Storage. Free quota applies to buckets in your Organization across regions.
  - *Expiry:* 90 days from activation; one-shot (cannot deactivate and resume).
  - *Gotchas:* The 2023 cut-off eligibility date is a hidden disqualifier for older accounts. Marketing pages say '750 GB/h free for 90 days' (GB per hour of trial, not 750GB/hour sustained) — it is 750 GB total. Data egress after trial follows regular pricing.
  - *Source:* https://www.scaleway.com/en/docs/object-storage/faq/
- **Ongoing free tiers: serverless monthly quotas, free Kapsule control plane, 1M free Generative API tokens** (high) — Recurring per-account free quotas on the official Serverless pricing page: Functions — 1,000,000 requests/month and 400,000 GB-s/month free; Containers — 400,000 GB-s/month and 200,000 vCPU-s/month free; Jobs — 400,000 GB-s/month and 200,000 vCPU-s/month free. Kapsule (managed Kubernetes) control plane is free (dedicated control-plane option is paid). Generative APIs: every new customer gets 1,000,000 free tokens (pay from the 1,000,001st). Docs billing concepts confirm free tiers on select products have no expiration date but may change.
  - *Eligibility:* Account-level: applies per account, resets monthly for the serverless quotas (Provisioned/warm resources have NO free tier and bill from the first second). No .edu requirement; Generative API token grant targets new customers.
  - *How to claim:* No application — quotas apply automatically to each account; Kapsule control plane is simply free when you create a cluster (nodes are billed from ~€0.027/h).
  - *Expiry:* Ongoing monthly resets (serverless), token grant is a one-time 1M-token welcome allowance; 'no expiration date but subject to change' per docs.
  - *Gotchas:* Free quotas cover compute/consumption only — data transfer (egress), provisioned instances, and storage attached to serverless workloads are billed. Prices are ex-VAT. These are not free VPS: the smallest Learning ('Stardust') instance is a paid ~€4.47/month box.
  - *Source:* https://www.scaleway.com/en/pricing/serverless/;https://www.scaleway.com/en/scaleway-vs-aws/(Kapsule);https://www.scaleway.com/en/generative-apis/(1Mtokens);https://www.scaleway.com/en/docs/billing/concepts/(free-tierpolicy)
- **Education / students: no real free credit program found** (medium) — No .edu-based free credit or education license program exists on Scaleway's official site as of today. The closest offerings are: (1) 'Learning Instances' (Stardust class: 1 shared vCPU, 1 GB RAM, 10 GB storage, 100 Mbps; available only in FR-PAR-1 and NL-AMS-1, max 1 per AZ, limited monthly supply) — cheap, not free; (2) Scaleway Learning — paid certification (€300 ex-VAT exam, badges valid 2 years). No university agreement or student voucher surfaced on official pages.
  - *Eligibility:* None education-specific. A student with a normal account gets the same offers as everyone (€100 trial requires Business type, 750 GB/90-day storage trial, monthly serverless free tiers).
  - *How to claim:* No student-only claim path exists; students use the standard new-account offers above.
  - *Gotchas:* Third-party 'student' listings (e.g., StudentPerks) only point back to the general startup program, not a student tier. Don't cite .edu eligibility that isn't documented.
  - *Source:* https://www.scaleway.com/en/scaleway-learning/;https://www.scaleway.com/fr/new-stardust-instances/(Englishequivalent:pricingforLearningInstancesunderVirtualInstances)

### UpCloud Free Trial

- **Standard 7-day free trial (quota-limited, no dollar credit)** (high) — All new users get a 7-day trial starting at the moment of activation, NOT a fixed credit pot. Trial quotas (official docs): Cloud Servers 2 cores / 4 GB RAM (all OSes except Windows); Block Storage 60 GB each of Archive, Standard and MaxIOPS; Networks 2 IPv4 + 2 IPv6; NAT/VPN Gateway x1 (Development or Standard plan); Managed Object Storage 1 instance up to 250 GB; Managed Databases 1 DB / 1 node (MySQL, PostgreSQL, Valkey, OpenSearch); Managed Load Balancer 1 Development plan, 1000 sessions; Managed Kubernetes 1 cluster on Development plan. Not available: Windows servers, GPU servers, Private Cloud. Firewall is locked to standard web ports (HTTP/HTTPS open both ways; SSH in only; RDP in only; SMTP 25 blocked both directions; NTP/DNS out only). You can redeploy within quotas as often as you like. Upgrade minimum is a one-time deposit of 10 in account currency; when the trial ends without a deposit, deployed services are removed and new deployments are blocked (account stays active). Data egress is free (zero-cost egress is a standard UpCloud feature since the 2025 'UpCloud Essentials' change).
  - *Eligibility:* All new users, no .edu, no region limit beyond UpCloud's sanctioned-region restrictions (Russia, Belarus, Crimea, Iran, North Korea, Cuba, Syria excluded). Credit card + billing info ARE required to activate the trial (0 or 1 EUR/USD temporary authorization, card not charged before/during/after). No card-free trial.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Register at signup.upcloud.com (name, email, phone required). 2) Log into the Control Panel; trial does not auto-start. 3) Verify identity with credit card + billing info to enter the trial. 4) Start trial within 365 days of registration or the account is removed. 5) Optionally pay min 10 one-time deposit to keep/upgrade; request longer trials from sales.
  - *Expiry:* 7 days from activation (longer only via promotion). Promo credits from signup codes are valid 60 days after signup per bonus-credits docs; trial credits for the standard trial effectively expire with the 7-day window. Accounts not started within 365 days of registration are deleted.
  - *Gotchas:* This is a quota trial, not money: the 'small ~$25 free trial credit' often cited for UpCloud does not match current docs - the baseline trial has no dollar credit at all. CC mandatory for activation. Windows/GPU excluded. After expiry your resources are deleted if you haven't deposited at least 10. SMTP port 25 blocked; outbound SSH restricted. 14-day and 30-day figures floating around are leftover promos, not the standard.
  - *Source:* https://upcloud.com/docs/getting-started/free-trial/(readdirectly2026-10-22)
- **30-day money-back guarantee on first payment (up to 500 EUR/USD)** (high) — The first payment made at/after trial upgrade carries a 30-day money-back guarantee of up to 500 EUR / 500 USD; contact support for refund. Not an independent offer - it attaches to the first one-time deposit (minimum 10).
  - *Eligibility:* Any new customer making their first payment; no .edu or startup requirements; same CC/sanctioned-region rules as the trial.
  - *How to claim:* Upgrade the trial account with a minimum 10 one-time deposit in Billing, use services for up to 30 days, request a refund from Customer Support within the window if unhappy.
  - *Expiry:* Refund window = 30 days from first payment, capped at 500 EUR/USD of the first payment.
  - *Gotchas:* Only the FIRST payment is covered and only up to 500; does not stack on top of promo credits. It is trial-vs-free: you must have paid to claim it.
  - *Source:* https://upcloud.com/docs/getting-started/free-trial/(readdirectly2026-10-22)
- **UpCloud Startup Program - up to 25,000 EUR credits in 3 tiers** (high) — Official program page (read directly today): Tier 1: up to 25,000 EUR free credit, valid 24 months, for startups in the UpCloud ecosystem (partner VC, incubator or accelerator) that have raised more than 5M EUR. Tier 2: 5,000 EUR free credit, valid 12 months, for startups founded within the last 24 months in FI, SE, NO, DK or IS (Nordics). Tier 3: 1,000 EUR free credit, valid 12 months, for startups founded within the last 24 months in non-Nordic EU, EEA, UK or CH. Credits usable across compute, storage, networking and managed databases; GPU Servers are excluded from credit usage (UpCloud may enable them at its discretion post-onboarding). Also includes 24/7 human support, startup events/forum, solution-architecture consultations, and a startup spotlight on UpCloud channels. Since March 2026 the program is run in partnership with Maria 01, the Helsinki startup hub. Applications reviewed in 5-10 business days; you may be asked for proof of company registration, valid VAT ID, funding-round documentation (Tier 1) and accelerator/VC/incubator affiliation proof (Tier 1). After credits expire, services continue at standard pricing and you are notified beforehand.
  - *Eligibility:* New UpCloud customers only; UpCloud account required before applying; company registered in EU, EEA, UK, Ukraine or CH; valid VAT ID; founded within the last 24 months (Tiers 2 and 3). Tier 1 additionally requires partner-ecosystem affiliation and >5M EUR raised. No .edu / student angle. No funding cap for T2/T3 (bootstrapped OK). Region-locked to Europe (Nordic advantage).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create an UpCloud account. 2) Open the Startup Program page and use the on-page 'Apply now' application form. 3) Submit company details; wait 5-10 business days for review. 4) On approval, credit is attached to your account (usage details in the welcome email).
  - *Expiry:* Tier 1 credits valid 24 months; Tier 2/3 valid 12 months from approval; program itself is ongoing (Maria 01 partnership announced March 2026).
  - *Gotchas:* Credits are denominated in EUR on the official page (site displays in euros; third-party trackers sometimes restate them in USD - treat EUR figures as canonical). 'Up to' Tier 1 means the final amount is UpCloud's discretion within the cap. GPU excluded. Must be a NEW customer - existing UpCloud users are ineligible. VAT ID is mandatory, so individuals/students without a company cannot use it. After expiry you pay standard prices with no cliff.
  - *Source:* https://upcloud.com/global/upcloud-startup-program/(readdirectly2026-10-22;Maria01:https://upcloud.com/global/blog/upcloud-partners-maria01-support-next-wave-finnish-european-startups/)
- **Signup promo-code trial credits (historically up to 500 EUR/USD for 30 days) - currently INACTIVE** (medium) — UpCloud layers time-boxed promos on the baseline trial: Aug 2025 'upcloud500' gave 500 USD for 30 days plus first-payment matching (max 100 USD, forever) and looser trial quotas (16 CPU / 32 GB / 1024 GB storage / 5 IPs), ending 2025-09-30; later 'boost500' offered 500 EUR or 500 USD valid for the first 30 days with GPU excluded. As of TODAY (2026-10-22) I verified on the live signup page that the boost500 code returns 'Promo code boost500 is inactive' and the default offer is the 7-day trial with an optional promo-code field. Affiliate codes for 250/500 credits also circulate but none are live on the default page. Per official bonus-credits docs: one promo code, only at signup, promo credits valid 60 days after signup, and promo-credit accounts need manual support activation to deploy GPU products.
  - *Eligibility:* New trial accounts; same CC-verification rules as the trial. No student/startup distinction - it is a general new-user promo.
  - *How to claim:* Register at signup.upcloud.com and enter a valid promo code in the 'Promo code' field at signup (only one code, only at signup). Check which code is live before relying on it - none are advertised on the default page today.
  - *Expiry:* boost500: INACTIVE as of 2026-10-22 (verified on signup page). upcloud500: ended 2025-09-30. Generic rule: promo credits valid 60 days after signup.
  - *Gotchas:* Inactivity of boost500 verified today; historical amounts/durations come from UpCloud's own social posts (Mastodon/Nostr) and third-party trackers, so treat the code details as second-hand. Codes expire without notice - recheck the signup page before claiming.
  - *Source:* https://signup.upcloud.com/(verifiedlive2026-10-22)andhttps://upcloud.com/docs/getting-started/billing/bonus-credits/(read2026-10-22);codesviaUpCloudsocials:https://mastodon.social/@UpCloud/115055129408699850
- **Referral program: 25 EUR/USD to the new user, 50 EUR/USD to the referrer** (high) — Per official bonus-credits docs (read today): a new user signing up via a referral receives 25 EUR / 25 USD free credits spendable on any service; the referrer earns 50 EUR / 50 USD on their account. Referral credits do NOT expire. Conditions: the referred new user must make a minimum of two one-time payments and stay active for at least 28 days for the referrer to receive the 50. So the new user's 25 requires two paid one-time deposits - effectively a paid-account perk, not free-for-forever.
  - *Eligibility:* Any new UpCloud user referred by an existing customer; no .edu or startup requirement; no stated funding/age cap; same sanctioned-region rules apply.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Get an existing customer's personal referral code/link. 2) Sign up with it. 3) New user receives 25 credit on the account. 4) Make at least two one-time payments and keep the account active 28+ days to unlock the referrer's 50.
  - *Expiry:* Referral credits do not expire (official docs).
  - *Gotchas:* You must actually pay (2 one-time deposits) - not pure free. One promo/referral code per signup, no stacking described. GPU deployment on bonus-credit accounts needs a support ticket to activate.
  - *Source:* https://upcloud.com/docs/getting-started/billing/bonus-credits/(read2026-10-22)
- **Education / students: no standing program found** (low) ⚠ low confidence: verify before claiming — No UpCloud education license, student tier, or .edu email benefit exists on the official site. The only education-related activity is ad-hoc: UpCloud has sponsored university cloud courses (case study: Stony Brook University's cloud computing class - the professor emails UpCloud each semester and UpCloud 'donates credit' to sponsor the whole class; documented 2023, not a published program with eligibility). Third-party coupon sites claim 'student discounts' but the FAQ they quote is generic filler, not an official offer.
  - *Eligibility:* Not available to students as a self-serve offer. Individual faculty can request class credit donations by contacting UpCloud directly.
  - *How to claim:* No public claim path. A professor/instructor contacts UpCloud sales/support to request sponsored credits for their class (per the Stony Brook example).
  - *Expiry:* N/A - no standing program; any credit donation is negotiated per course/semester.
  - *Source:* https://upcloud.com/global/blog/stony-brook-university-uses-upcloud-educate-future-cloud-developers/
- **2025–26 change:** 1) The task's starting URL https://upcloud.com/try/ now serves a 404 'Page Not Found' (verified 2026-10-22) - UpCloud has sunset the dedicated trial landing page; trial info now lives in the docs (upcloud.com/docs/getting-started/free-trial/) and the signup flow. 2) The '500 EUR/USD for 30 days' signup promo chain (upcloud500 in 2025, then boost500 active into Sept 2026) is dead: the live signup page today shows boost500 as 'inactive' and defaults to the plain 7-day quota trial. So any listing advertising a standing $500/30-day free trial (e.g. 'UpCloud for Startups: $500' directory entries) is outdated as of 2026-10-22. 3) The previously circulated 'small ~$25 free trial credit' assumption is wrong for current terms - the baseline trial is quota-based with no dollar credit; 25 EUR only appears via the referral program. 4) The Startup Program is the main 2025-2026 structural change: it now runs a three-tier EUR system (up to 25k/5k/1k, 24/12/12-month validity) gated on VAT ID + 24-month company age + EU/EEA/UK/UA/CH registration, formalized in a March 2026 Maria 01 (Helsinki) partnership; third-party trackers restate tiers in USD - the official page prices in EUR. 5) Since June 2025, 'UpCloud Essentials' made zero-cost egress, Kubernetes control planes, load balancers, NAT/VPN gateways and a daily backup plan permanently free for all users - not a trial offer, but it removes the usual egress-cost gotcha. 6) Unverified third-party claim (notes.aflukasz.pl, May 2025): 5,000 EUR for European businesses with a VAT ID, usable over 3 months - not corroborated on the official site; treat as a lapsed enterprise promo. 7) Region limits: UpCloud does not serve sanctioned regions (RU, BY, Crimea, IR, KP, CU, SY) and validates payment origin/geo - all offers above are subject to that.

### Vultr Free Trial

- **New-account trial credits (up to 30 days, amount promo-tied)** (high) — Official docs state 'Vultr offers free credits for up to 30 days for you to try our services.' No dollar amount is published in official docs; the amount is tied to the promo link used (e.g. try100 = $100). Payment method required for verification, but card linking works with $0.00 deposit — no prepay forced. Credit card NOT required in the strict sense: linking only, $0 charge (temporary auth hold possible). Applies to all products (trial covers general services; GPU applicability not officially stated).
  - *Eligibility:* New accounts only; valid email; payment-method verification required; no region limit stated; no .edu or startup requirement
  - *How to claim:* Register at vultr.com (promo/try100 links auto-attach trial credit), verify email, add payment method — a credit card can be linked with the 'I just want to link my credit card - $0.00 deposit' option (bank may place a temporary auth hold), optionally apply a promo coupon in Billing > Summary, accept ToS. Deprovision resources before credits expire to avoid charges.
  - *Expiry:* Credits valid up to 30 days from registration (per docs 'up to 30 days'); unused credit is forfeited at expiry; no renewal possible on the same account
  - *Gotchas:* Amount not officially fixed — whatever the promo link grants (commonly $100, promos $200-$300). Credit is not a spending cap: running instances keep billing after credit runs out or the 30 days elapse. Stopped (not destroyed) instances still accrue charges. Data egress beyond plan allowance, snapshots, and extra IPs bill extra. Duplicate accounts not eligible. The exact dollar amount for a bare try100 signup is not stated on the official docs page.
  - *Source:* https://docs.vultr.com/platform/create-an-account
- **Promo signup links (try100 / try250 trial pages)** (medium) — Vultr runs per-page signup promos: https://www.vultr.com/promo/try100/ (historically $100 credit) and https://www.vultr.com/promo/try250 ($250). Both pages were live/indexed as of late 2026 (try250 confirmed in Oct 2026 crawl of vultr.com, now listing 34 regions). The promo page returns 403 to automated fetchers, so the exact on-page credit figure couldn't be read directly today; amount is tied to the link and can change.
  - *Eligibility:* New Vultr customers only; duplicate accounts excluded; payment method required; no .edu or startup age requirement
  - *How to claim:* Sign up through the specific promo page (vultr.com/promo/try100 or /try250) instead of the plain signup; credit attaches to the new account and expires ~30 days after registration; no coupon code needed.
  - *Expiry:* Trial credit valid 30 days after registration (consistent with docs 'up to 30 days')
  - *Gotchas:* Promo credit amount varies by link and over time; a copied old link may attach a different amount. CC needed for verification ($0-deposit card link allowed). Verify the actual credit in Billing before deploying. Non-stackable with other promos.
  - *Source:* https://www.vultr.com/promo/try250
- **Official coupon codes: $200 / $250 / $300 trial credits, VULTRMATCH, 50% off** (medium) — Vultr's official coupons page (Oct 2026 listings confirmed by a 2026-10-06 check): FLYTWOHUNDRED = $200; 250VULTRFLY = $250; FLY300VULTR = $300; VULTRMATCH = match first deposit dollar-for-dollar up to $100 (credit expires 12 months after issuance; applied 50/50 — $5 promo + $5 real per $10 of hourly spend); 50off page = 50% off first two invoices (invoices generated on the 1st monthly). New customers only; duplicate accounts not eligible; offers non-combinable ('limited time').
  - *Eligibility:* New customers only; valid credit card or PayPal for account verification; no .edu or startup requirement; no stated region limit
  - *How to claim:* Create a new account, then apply the code in Billing > Summary (or on the promo landing page). VULTRMATCH: make a first deposit (minimum deposit required by the page); credits auto-match 50/50. 50off: just sign up new and the discount applies to first two invoices. Check the exact code list at vultr.com/coupons before use.
  - *Expiry:* Standard $200/$250/$300 trial credits: ~30 days. VULTRMATCH match credit: 12 months. 50% discount: first two monthly invoices only. Promos explicitly 'limited time'.
  - *Gotchas:* The coupons page itself is bot-blocked (403) — codes rotate; verify which are live at vultr.com/coupons. Larger credit usually means longer minimum deposit or tighter product eligibility. 50off is a discount, not free credit. If card verification is denied, a small deposit (~$5) may be required to unlock credits.
  - *Source:* https://www.vultr.com/coupons/
- **Free Tier Program — free 1vCPU/512MB/10GB SSD instance** (high) — Genuinely free instance (not a trial): 1 vCPU / 512 MB RAM / 10 GB SSD / 1 IPv4. Available only in Miami, Seattle, and Frankfurt data centers. 'Anyone is eligible to apply'; admission is randomized using a per-account weighted score computed during identity/usage validation; limited quantities, rolled out in phases. No hard dates or end date published; no duration limit on the instance itself is stated (adjustments made 'as we go').
  - *Eligibility:* Open to anyone (no .edu, no funding cap, no age limit stated); real-person validation required (anti-bot); credit card NOT required; region availability limited to Miami/Seattle/Frankfurt only
  - *How to claim:* Log in or sign up at vultr.com, apply for early access on the Free Tier Program page (vultr.com/free-tier-program/); provide personal info and intended use to raise weighted score; wait for email/console admission notice; deploy under Cloud Compute > Regular > Free Instance.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry stated; program is phase-rolled and 'fine-tuned as we go' — instances could be affected by program changes; no renewal concept (it is a permanent free instance for admitted accounts)
  - *Gotchas:* Admission is not guaranteed (randomized + weighted score); you simply may not be admitted — Vultr does not say why. Free tier instance is a 512MB/1vCPU/10GB plan, not the full product lineup. Availability in just 3 regions. Verify the instance still appears on the deploy page (Cloud Compute > Regular > Free Instance); admission notified by email + console banner.
  - *Source:* https://www.vultr.com/free-tier-program/
- **Student .edu email $5 credit (legacy)** (low) ⚠ low confidence: verify before claiming — Long-claimed offer: new accounts registered with a .edu email get a $5 free credit. Documented only by third-party coupon sites (dealsvalid ~2023 'Get $5 Vultr Credit for Free', vpscoupon 2019 'Get $50... .edu gets $5', resourify which calls it 'the older student-email offer'). Not listed on Vultr's official coupons, docs, or free-tier pages in any 2025-2026 snapshot found; Vultr runs no dedicated student program page.
  - *Eligibility:* As claimed: students/anyone with a valid .edu email from an accredited US university; new account; CC verification likely required for the account generally
  - *How to claim:* Register a new Vultr account using a .edu email from an accredited US university; the $5 credit is attached automatically (per third-party write-ups, no coupon code needed).
  - *Expiry:* Unknown; not stated anywhere official
  - *Gotchas:* Unverifiable as a current official offer in 2026 — may be a legacy/abandoned promotion; do not plan around it. If real, $5 is small (about one month of a $5 plan). Test only by signing up; check Billing for the credit. No region limit known.
  - *Source:* https://www.dealsvalid.com/brands/vultr
- **Vultr Digital Start-up Program (up to $100K credits + 35% discounts)** (high) — Up to $100K in cloud credits plus up to 35% long-term discounts, executive sponsorship, dedicated account management, architecture reviews, and priority support. Eligibility: must have secured recent Series A, B, C, D, or E financing (no company-age threshold published). Application requires: senior-most technical executive contact (CTO), senior-most cloud-infrastructure lead contact, last 6 months of cloud-compute invoices, and agreement to be publicly referenceable as a program member.
